### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Romania)



[Diplomatic missions of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_missions_of_Romania)&#x20;

&#x20; Countries where Romania operates at least one [diplomatic mission](8970.md) ([embassy](8970.md), [consulate](6770059.md), or [representative office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_office)).

&#x20; Other sovereign states and territories with no Romanian diplomatic presence.

&#x20; Romania

Since December 1989, Romania has pursued a policy of strengthening relations with the West in general, more specifically with the [United States](3434750.md) and the [EU](9317.md), albeit with limited [relations involving](16485065.md) the [Russian Federation](25391.md). It joined [NATO](21133.md) on 29 March 2004, the EU on 1 January 2007, while it joined the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) and the [World Bank](36752.md) in 1972, and is a founding member of the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization).[^cite_note-210] Romania is recognised as a [middle power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_power) for its [military capabilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_level_of_military_equipment), as well as its active diplomatic engagement on the global stage.[^cite_note-HCSSABalancingAct-211][^cite_note-StuckInMiddleGear-212]

Recent governments have stated that their goals include strengthening ties with and helping other countries (in particular [Moldova](19260.md), [Ukraine](31750.md), and [Georgia](48768.md)) with better integration with the rest of the [West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_European_Politics).[^cite_note-mae-213] Romania has also made clear since the late 1990s that it supports NATO and EU membership for the democratic former [Soviet republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republics_of_the_Soviet_Union) in [Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe) and the [Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caucasus).

Romania applied to join to the [Schengen Area](16565139.md) in 2007, [acquiring full membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Bulgaria_and_Romania_to_the_Schengen_Area) in 2025 along with Bulgaria.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215] In December 2005, President [Traian Băsescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traian_B%C4%83sescu) and [United States Secretary of State](32293.md) [Condoleezza Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condoleezza_Rice) signed an agreement that would allow a U.S. military presence at several Romanian facilities primarily in the eastern part of the country.[^cite_note-216] In 2009, US Secretary of State [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) referred to Romania as "one of the most trustworthy and respectable US allies".[^cite_note-217] However, by 2025, relations had worsened, with US vice president [JD Vance](53396477.md) in February [scolding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_JD_Vance_speech_at_the_Munich_Security_Conference) "[flimsy suspicions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accusations_of_Russian_interference_in_the_2024_Romanian_presidential_election)" and "enormous pressure from its continental neighbours" for causing the annulment of the [2024 Romanian presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Romanian_presidential_election) election in which [Călin Georgescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83lin_Georgescu) won the first round.

[Relations with Moldova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian-Moldovan_relations) are a special case given that the two countries share the same language and a [common history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Moldavia).[^cite_note-mae-213] A [movement for unification of Moldova and Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_for_unification_of_Moldova_and_Romania) appeared in the early 1990s after both countries achieved emancipation from [communist rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state)[^cite_note-cfis-218] but lost ground in the mid-1990s when a new Moldovan government pursued an agenda towards preserving a Moldovan republic independent of Romania.[^cite_note-Ihrig-219] After the [2009 protests in Moldova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Moldovan_protests) and the subsequent removal of Communists from power, relations between the two countries have improved considerably.[^cite_note-220]

### Military

Main articles: [Romanian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Armed_Forces) and [Military history of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Romania)



Romanian soldiers at the Saber Guardian 23 exercise opening ceremony in [Smârdan, Galați](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sm%C3%A2rdan,_Gala%C8%9Bi)



An TR-85 M1 tank at the 2007 Romanian military parade for national day, with the Arch of Triumph in the background

The Romanian Armed Forces consist of [land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Land_Forces), [air](2106520.md), and [naval forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Naval_Forces) led by a [Commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Romanian_General_Staff) under the supervision of the [Ministry of National Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defence_\(Romania\)), and by the [president](273659.md) as the Supreme Commander during wartime. The Armed Forces consist of approximately 55,000 reservists and 71,500 active military personnel—35,800 for land, 10,700 for air, 6,600 for naval forces, and 16,500 in other fields.[^cite_note-military_balance_2022-221] Total defence spending in 2023 accounted for 2.44% of total national GDP, or approximately US\$8.48 billion,[^cite_note-222] with a total of \$9 billion intended to be spent until 2026 for modernisation and acquisition of new equipment.[^cite_note-223] Conscription stopped in 2007, when Romania switched to a volunteer army.

The Air Force operates [F-16AM/BM MLU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon_variants#F-16AM/BM_Block_15_MLU) fighters,[^cite_note-224] [C-27J Spartan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-27J_Spartan) and [C-130 Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130_Hercules) [transport aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_transport_aircraft), as well as [IAR 330](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAR_330) and [IAR 316](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAR_316) helicopters.[^cite_note-225] A [procurement programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Romania) for [F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) [fifth-generation fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth-generation_fighter) is also currently being carried out.[^cite_note-226] The Naval Forces operate three frigates, of which two are [Type 22 frigates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_22_frigate) acquired from the British [Royal Navy](26061.md),[^cite_note-awst_20061211-227] as well as four corvettes. The [River Flotilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Danube_Flotilla) operates [Mihail Kogălniceanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihail_Kog%C4%83lniceanu-class_river_monitor) and [Smârdan-class river monitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sm%C3%A2rdan-class_river_monitor).[^cite_note-military_balance_2022-221]

Romania contributed troops to the international coalition in [Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) beginning in 2002,[^cite_note-228] with a peak deployment of 1,600 troops in 2010 (which was the 4th largest contribution according to the US).[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] Its combat mission in the country concluded in 2014.[^cite_note-231] Romanian troops participated in the [occupation of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Iraq_\(2003%E2%80%932011\)), reaching a peak of 730 soldiers before being slowly drawn down to 350 soldiers. Romania terminated its mission in [Iraq](7515928.md) and withdrew its last troops on 24 July 2009, among the last countries to do so. The frigate the *[Regele Ferdinand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regele_Ferdinand_frigate)* participated in the [2011 military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya).[^cite_note-232]

In December 2011, the [Romanian Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_Romania) unanimously adopted the draft law ratifying the [Romania-United States agreement](15994235.md) signed in September of the same year that would allow the establishment and operation of a US land-based [ballistic missile defence system](249526.md) in Romania as part of NATO's efforts to build a continental [missile shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_missile_defence_system).[^cite_note-233] The [Aegis Ashore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegis_Ashore) missile system based at [Deveslu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deveselu_Military_Base) became operational in 2016.[^cite_note-234]

In 2024, construction work started on expanding the Mihail Kogălniceanu Air Base ([RoAF 57th Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RoAF_57th_Air_Base)). The air base is set to become the largest [NATO](21133.md) base in [Europe](9239.md) after the implementation of a project spanning 20 years.[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Romania)

In 2024, Romania had a [GDP](12594.md) (PPP) of around \$894 billion and a [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_per_capita_\(PPP\)) ([PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)) of \$47,203.[^cite_note-IMFWEO.RO-7] According to the [World Bank](36752.md), Romania is a [high-income economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-income_economy).[^cite_note-WB_GROUP-237] According to [Eurostat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostat), Romania's GDP per capita (PPS) was 77% of the EU average (100%) in 2022, an increase from 44% in 2007 (the year of Romania's accession to the EU), making Romania one of the [fastest growing economies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_real_GDP_growth_rate) in the [EU](9317.md).[^cite_note-238]

The [Bucharest Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucharest_Stock_Exchange) (BVB) is the [stock exchange](28168.md) of Romania, located in [Bucharest](36877.md). In 2024, the BVB boasted a \$74 billion [market capitalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalisation) and a [trading volume](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trading_volume) of \$7.2 billion.[^cite_note-General_statistics-239] As of 2024, 86 companies were listed on the exchange.[^cite_note-General_statistics-239] In September 2020, [FTSE Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FTSE_Russell) upgraded the BVB from a [Frontier market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_market) to a [Secondary Emerging Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_market).[^cite_note-240]



[Floreasca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floreasca) business district, as seen from [Lake Herăstrău](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Her%C4%83str%C4%83u)

After 1989 the country experienced a decade of economic instability and decline, led in part by an obsolete [industrial base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing) and a lack of [structural reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-reformist_reform). From 2000 onwards, however, the Romanian economy was transformed into one of relative [macroeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroeconomic) stability, characterised by [high growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_growth), low [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment) and declining [inflation](38286.md). In 2006, according to the [Romanian Statistics Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_\(Romania\)), GDP growth in real terms was recorded at 7.7%, one of the highest rates in [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-241] However, the [Great Recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession) forced the government to borrow externally, including an [IMF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMF) €20 billion bailout programme.[^cite_note-242] According to [The World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Bank), GDP per capita in [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) grew from \$13,703 in 2007 to \$47,903 in 2023.[^cite_note-243]

&#x20;\
&#x20;Romania's main exports are [vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicles), software, clothing and textiles, [industrial machinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_industry), [electrical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_device) and [electronic equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_equipment), [metallurgic products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metallurgy), [raw materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raw_materials), [military equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_equipment), [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), [fine chemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_chemicals), and [agricultural products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture) ([fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruit), [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetable), and [flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flower)). Trade is mostly centred on the member states of the [EU](9317.md), with [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md) and [France](5843419.md) being the country's single largest [trading partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_trade).

In 2005, the government replaced Romania's [progressive tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_tax) system with a [flat tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_tax) of 16% for both [personal income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_income) and [corporate profit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Profiteering), among the lowest rates in the EU.[^cite_note-244] The economy is based predominantly on [services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_\(economics\)), which account for 56.2% of the country's total GDP as of 2017, with [industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_in_Romania) and [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture) accounting for 30% and 4.4% respectively.[^cite_note-245] Approximately 25.8% of the Romanian workforce is employed in agriculture, one of the highest rates in [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-246]



Romania is home to automobile company [Dacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile_Dacia)

Romania has attracted increasing amounts of foreign investment following the end of [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism), with the stock of [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment) (FDI) in Romania rising to €83.8 billion in June 2019.[^cite_note-business-review\.eu-247] Romania's FDI outward stock (an external or foreign business either investing in or purchasing the stock of a local economy) amounted to \$745 million in December 2018, the lowest value among the 28 [EU member states](10890716.md).[^cite_note-business-review\.eu-247]

Since 1867 the official currency has been the [Romanian *leu*](642267.md) ("lion"), which "was [redenominated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redenomination), with the exchange rate set at 1 new leu = 10,000 old lei" in 2005 according to the [National Bank of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Romania).[^cite_note-248] As it joined the EU in 2007, Romania plans to adopt the [euro](9472.md) in 2029.[^cite_note-249]

### Infrastructure

Main articles: [Transport in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Romania) and [Energy in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Romania)



Romania's [road network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_Romania)



Graph depicting Romania's electricity supply mix as of 2015

According to the [Romania's National Institute of Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_\(Romania\)) (INS), Romania's total road network was estimated in 2015 at 86,080 kilometres (53,488 mi).[^cite_note-INSSE-250] The [World Bank](36752.md) estimates the railway network at 22,298 kilometres (13,855 mi) of track, the fourth-largest [railway network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Romania) in [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-Railway-251] Romania's [rail transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83ile_Ferate_Rom%C3%A2ne) experienced a dramatic decline after 1989 and was estimated at 99 million passenger journeys in 2004, but has experienced a recent (2013) revival due to infrastructure improvements and partial privatisation of lines,[^cite_note-Europaworld-199] accounting for 45% of all [passenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passenger) and [freight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cargo) movements in the country.[^cite_note-Europaworld-199] [Bucharest Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucharest_Metro), the only [underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) railway system, was opened in 1979 and measures 80.01 km (49.72 mi) with an average ridership in 2021 of 720,000 passengers during the workweek in the country.[^cite_note-252] There are [sixteen international commercial airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Romania) in service today. Over 12.8 million passengers flew through Bucharest's [Henri Coandă International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Coand%C4%83_International_Airport) in 2017.[^cite_note-253]

Romania is a net exporter of electrical energy and is 52nd worldwide in terms of consumption of [electric energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_energy).[^cite_note-254] Around a third of the produced energy comes from [renewable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_resource), mostly as [hydroelectric power](381399.md).[^cite_note-255] It has one of the largest [refining capacities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refining) in [Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe), even though [oil](4207510.md) and [natural gas](22131.md) production has been decreasing for more than a decade.[^cite_note-256] With one of the largest reserves of [crude oil](23195.md) and [shale gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas) in Europe[^cite_note-257] it is among the most energy-independent countries in the EU,[^cite_note-258] and is looking to expand its [nuclear power plant](207347.md) at [Cernavodă](3844451.md) further.[^cite_note-259]

There were almost 18.3 million connections to the [Internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet) in June 2014.[^cite_note-260] According to [Bloomberg](432006.md), in 2013 Romania ranked fifth in the world, and according to *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)*, it ranks number one in [Europe at Internet speeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet-speed_development),[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] with [Timișoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timi%C8%99oara) ranked among the highest in the world.[^cite_note-Akamai-263]

### Tourism

Main articles: [Tourism in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Romania) and [List of World Heritage Sites in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Romania)

See also: [Seven Natural Wonders of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Natural_Wonders_of_Romania) and [Seven Wonders of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Wonders_of_Romania)



[Putna Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putna_Monastery) in [Bukovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukovina), one of the [medieval churches of Moldavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churches_of_Moldavia)



The [Danube Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danube_Delta) with its wildlife



[Bran Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bran_Castle)



[Mamaia Black Sea resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Black_Sea_resorts)

Tourism is a significant contributor to the Romanian economy, generating around 5% of GDP.[^cite_note-264] The number of tourists has been rising steadily, reaching 9.33 million foreign tourists in 2016, according to the [Worldbank](36752.md).[^cite_note-business_review_website-265] Tourism in Romania attracted €400 million in investments in 2005.[^cite_note-266] More than 60% of the foreign visitors in 2007 were from other EU countries.[^cite_note-turism-267] The popular summer attractions of [Mamaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamaia) and other [Black Sea Resorts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Black_Sea_resorts) attracted 1.3 million tourists in 2009.[^cite_note-litoral2010-08-21-268][^cite_note-269]

Among Romania's [spa towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa_town) and [resorts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resort) are [Buziaș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buzia%C8%99), [Băile Călacea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Or%C8%9Bi%C8%99oara), [Moneasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moneasa), [Băile Lipova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipova,_Arad), [Călimănești](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83lim%C4%83ne%C8%99ti), [Amara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amara,_Romania), and [Geoagiu-Băi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoagiu).[^cite_note-270] At the same time, [seaside resorts](293990.md) such as [Eforie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eforie), [Mangalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangalia), [Saturn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturn,_Romania), [Venus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venus,_Romania), [Neptun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptun,_Romania), [Olimp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olimp,_Romania), and [Mamaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamaia) (sometimes also called [the Romanian Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Black_Sea_resorts)) are among the main tourist attractions during the [summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer). Most popular [skiing resorts](179821.md) are along the [Valea Prahovei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valea_Prahovei), [Muntele Mic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C8%9Aarcu_Mountains), [Semenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banat_Mountains), [Straja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straja) and [Poiana Brașov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poiana_Bra%C8%99ov). [Wine tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wine_tourism) is represented by areas such as [Recaș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reca%C8%99), Silagiu, [Odobești](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odobe%C8%99ti), [Drăgășani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr%C4%83g%C4%83%C8%99ani), and Podgoria Aradului.[^cite_note-271]

[Castles, fortifications, or strongholds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_castles_in_Romania) as well as preserved medieval Transylvanian cities or towns such as [Timișoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timi%C8%99oara), [Cluj-Napoca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluj-Napoca), [Sibiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sibiu), [Brașov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bra%C8%99ov), [Alba Iulia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alba_Iulia), [Baia Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baia_Mare), [Bistrița](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bistri%C8%9Ba), [Mediaș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media%C8%99), [Cisnădie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisn%C4%83die), [Sebeș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebe%C8%99), or [Sighișoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sighi%C8%99oara) also attract a large number of tourists. [Bran Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bran_Castle), near Brașov, is one of the most famous attractions in Romania, drawing hundreds of thousands of tourists every year as it is often (falsely) advertised as being [Dracula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dracula_in_popular_culture)'s Castle.[^cite_note-272] Other attractions include the [Danube Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danube_Delta) or the [Sculptural Ensemble of Constantin Brâncuși at Târgu Jiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sculptural_Ensemble_of_Constantin_Br%C3%A2ncu%C8%99i_at_T%C3%A2rgu_Jiu).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274]

[Rural tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_tourism), focusing on getting visitors acquainted with local [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore) and [customs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tradition), has become an important alternative,[^cite_note-275] and is targeted to promote such sites as [Bran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bran,_Bra%C8%99ov) and its Dracula's Castle, the [painted churches of northern Moldavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_churches_of_northern_Moldavia), and the [wooden churches of Maramureș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wooden_churches_of_Maramure%C8%99), or the [villages with fortified churches in Transylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villages_with_fortified_churches_in_Transylvania).[^cite_note-276] The [Via Transilvanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Via_Transilvanica) long-distance hiking and cycling [trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail), which crosses 10 counties in the [Transylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transylvania), [Banat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banat) and [Bukovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukovina) regions of the country further promotes rural [slow tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slow_tourism).[^cite_note-277] Other important [itineraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_itinerary) include Via Mariae, [EuroVelo 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EV6_The_Rivers_Route) and [13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EV13_The_Iron_Curtain_Trail), etc.[^cite_note-278]

In 2014, Romania had 32,500 companies active in the hotel and restaurant industry, with a total turnover of €2.6 billion.[^cite_note-279] More than 1.9 million foreign tourists visited Romania in 2014, 12% more than in 2013.[^cite_note-romania-insider.com-280] According to the country's [National Statistics Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_\(Romania\)), some 77% came from [Europe](9239.md) (particularly from [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), and [France](5843419.md)), 12% from [Asia](689.md), and less than 7% from [North America](21139.md).[^cite_note-romania-insider.com-280]

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Romania) and [List of Romanian inventors and discoverers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Romanian_inventors_and_discoverers)

Historically, Romanian researchers and inventors have made notable contributions to several fields. In the history of flight, [Traian Vuia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traian_Vuia) built the first [aeroplane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeroplane) to take off under its own power[^cite_note-RomAcademyLib-281] and [Aurel Vlaicu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurel_Vlaicu) built and flew some of the earliest successful aircraft,[^cite_note-282] while [Henri Coandă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Coand%C4%83) discovered the [Coandă effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coand%C4%83_effect) of fluidics.[^cite_note-283] [Victor Babeș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Babe%C8%99) discovered more than 50 types of bacteria;[^cite_note-284] biologist [Nicolae Paulescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Paulescu) developed an extract of the pancreas and showed that it lowers blood sugar in diabetic dogs, thus being significant in the history of [insulin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insulin);[^cite_note-285] while [Emil Palade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emil_Palade) received the [Nobel Prize](21201.md) for his contributions to [cell biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cell_biology).[^cite_note-286] [Lazăr Edeleanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laz%C4%83r_Edeleanu) was the first chemist to synthesise [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine), and he also invented the procedure of separating valuable petroleum components with selective solvents.[^cite_note-287]

During the 1990s and 2000s, the development of research was hampered by several factors, including [corruption](3608404.md), low [funding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funding), and a considerable [brain drain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain_drain).[^cite_note-288] In recent years, Romania has ranked the lowest or second-lowest in the EU by [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) spending as a percentage of GDP, standing at roughly 0.5% in 2016 and 2017, substantially below the EU average of just over 2%.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] The country joined the [European Space Agency](10363.md) (ESA) in 2011,[^cite_note-291] and [CERN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERN) in 2016.[^cite_note-292] In 2018, however, Romania lost its voting rights in the ESA due to a failure to pay €56.8 million in membership contributions to the agency.[^cite_note-293]

In the early 2010s, the situation for science in Romania was characterised as "rapidly improving" albeit from a low base.[^cite_note-294] In January 2011, Parliament passed a law that enforces "strict quality control on universities and introduces tough rules for funding evaluation and peer review".[^cite_note-Abbott2011-295] Romania was ranked 49th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297]

The [nuclear physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_physics) facility of the EU's proposed [Extreme Light Infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_Light_Infrastructure) (ELI) [laser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser) will be built in Romania.[^cite_note-298] In early 2012, Romania launched its first [satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goliat) from the [Centre Spatial Guyanais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_Spatial_Guyanais) in French Guiana.[^cite_note-299] Starting in December 2014, Romania became a co-owner of the [International Space Station](15043.md).[^cite_note-300]

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Romania) and [Romanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanians)

See also: [Demographic history of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Romania), [Immigration to Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Romania), and [Minorities in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minorities_in_Romania)



Romanians by counties (Ethnic maps 1930–2021)

According to the [2021 Romanian census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Romanian_census), Romania's population was 19,053,815.[^cite_note-Census2021-6] Like other countries in the region, its population is expected to decline gradually as a result of [sub-replacement fertility rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility_rate) and negative [net migration rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate). According to the 2021 Romanian census, [Romanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanians) made up 89.33% of the population, [Hungarians](17106358.md) 6.05% and the [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) 3.44% of the population,[^cite_note-Census2021-6] but many ethnicities are not recorded, as they do not have [ID cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_document).[^cite_note-301] International sources give higher figures for Roma than the official census.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] According to the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), the Roma make up 8.32% of the population;[^cite_note-305] this figure is difficult to verify due to the mobility of Roma and the reluctance of some of them to disclose their ethnicity.[^cite_note-306] Hungarians constitute a majority in the counties of [Harghita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harghita) and [Covasna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covasna_County). Other minorities include [Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainians_of_Romania), [Germans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germans_of_Romania), [Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turks_of_Romania), [Lipovans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipovans), [Aromanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanians_in_Romania), [Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars_of_Romania), and [Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbs_of_Romania).[^cite_note-census-307] In 1930, there were 745,421 Germans living in Romania,[^cite_note-308] but only about 36,000 remained in the country to this day.[^cite_note-census-307] As of 2009, there were also approximately 133,000 immigrants living in Romania, primarily from [Moldova](19260.md) and [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-165]

The [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) (TFR) in 2018 was estimated at 1.36 children born per woman, which is below the replacement rate of 2.1, and one of the lowest in the world;[^cite_note-cia.gov1-309] it remains considerably below the high of 5.82 children born per woman in 1912.[^cite_note-310] In 2014, 31.2% of births were to unmarried women.[^cite_note-311] The [birth rate](515898.md) (9.49‰, 2012) is much lower than the [mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortality_rate) (11.84‰, 2012), resulting in a shrinking (−0.26% per year, 2012) and aging population (median age: 41.6 years, 2018), one of the oldest populations in the world,[^cite_note-cia.gov1-309] with approximately 16.8% of total population aged 65 years and over.[^cite_note-cia.gov1-309][^cite_note-populationdata.net-312][^cite_note-313] The life expectancy in 2015 was estimated at 74.92 years (71.46 years male, 78.59 years female).[^cite_note-cia.gov-314] The number of Romanians and individuals with ancestors born in Romania living abroad is estimated at 12 million.[^cite_note-diaspora-315] After the [Romanian Revolution of 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Revolution_of_1989), a significant number of Romanians emigrated to other [European countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_in_Europe), [North America](21139.md) or [Australia](4689264.md).[^cite_note-316] For example, in 1990, 96,919 Romanians permanently settled abroad.[^cite_note-317]

### Urbanisation

Main articles: [List of cities and towns in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Romania) and [Metropolitan areas in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_areas_in_Romania)

Although 54.0% of the population lived in [urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_areas) in 2011,[^cite_note-Census2011-318] this percentage has been declining since 1996.[^cite_note-319] Counties with over 2⁄3 urban population are [Hunedoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunedoara_County), [Brașov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bra%C8%99ov_County) and [Constanța](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constan%C8%9Ba_County), while those with less than a third are [Dâmbovița](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%A2mbovi%C8%9Ba_County) (30.06%) and [Giurgiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giurgiu_County) and [Teleorman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teleorman_County).[^cite_note-Census2011-318] Bucharest is the capital and the largest city in Romania, with a population of over 1.7 million in 2021.[^cite_note-RPL2021-320] Its [larger urban zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larger_urban_zone) has a population of almost 2.2 million,[^cite_note-321] which are planned to be included into a [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucharest_metropolitan_area) up to 20 times the area of the [city proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_proper).[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]

Another 17 cities have a population of over 100,000, with Cluj-Napoca, Iași, Constanța and Timișoara having more than 250,000 inhabitants, and Craiova, Brașov and Galați with over 200,000 inhabitants.[^cite_note-RPL2021-320] [Metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_areas_in_Romania) have been constituted for most of these cities.&#x20;

| 9 | [Oradea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oradea) | [Bihor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bihor_County) | 183,105 | 19 | [Râmnicu Vâlcea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A2mnicu_V%C3%A2lcea) | [Vâlcea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%A2lcea_County) | 93,151 |
| 10 | [Ploiești](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ploie%C8%99ti) | [Prahova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prahova_County) | 180,540 | 20 | [Satu Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satu_Mare) | [Satu Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satu_Mare_County) | 91,520 |

### Languages

Main articles: [Romanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_language) and [Languages of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Romania)



Map highlighting the use of the Romanian language worldwide, both as a native and as a foreign language

The official language is Romanian, a [Romance language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_language) (the most widely spoken of the [Eastern Romance branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Romance_languages)), which presents a consistent degree of similarity to [Aromanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanian_language), [Megleno-Romanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megleno-Romanian_language), and [Istro-Romanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istro-Romanian_language), but shares many features equally with the rest of the [Western Romance languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Romance_languages), specifically [Italian](14708.md), [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language), [Spanish](26825.md), [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language), and [Catalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catalan_language).[^cite_note-Parolando-326] The [Romanian alphabet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_alphabet) contains the same 26 letters of the standard Latin alphabet, as well as five additional ones (namely *ă*, *â*, *î*, *ț*, and *ș*), totalling 31.[^cite_note-Parolando-326]

Romanian is spoken as a first language by 91.55% of the entire population, while [Hungarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_language) and [Vlax Romani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlax_Romani) are spoken by 6.28% and 1.20% of the population, respectively. There are also 40,861 native speakers of [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) (concentrated in some compact regions near the border, where they form local majorities),[^cite_note-infomm.ro_2015-05-05-327] 17,101 native speakers of [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_language), 15,943 native speakers of [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language), and 14,414 native speakers of [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) living in Romania.[^cite_note-Census2021-Language-328][^cite_note-census_2011_lang-329]

According to the Constitution, local councils ensure linguistic rights to all minorities. In localities with ethnic minorities of over 20%, that minority's language can be used in the public administration, justice system, and education. Foreign citizens and stateless persons who live in Romania have access to justice and education in their own language.[^cite_note-330] [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) and [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language) are the main foreign languages taught in schools.[^cite_note-331] In 2010, the [Organisation internationale de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie) identified 4,756,100 French speakers in the country.[^cite_note-332] According to the 2012 [Eurobarometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobarometer), English is spoken by 31% of Romanians, French is spoken by 17%, and [Italian](14708.md) and [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language), each by 7%.[^cite_note-333]

### Religion

Main articles: [Religion in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Romania) and [Romanian Orthodox Church](384458.md)



[The People's Salvation Cathedral](1281047.md) in [Bucharest](36877.md) is the [tallest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_Eastern_Orthodox_church_buildings) and [largest Eastern Orthodox church building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_church_buildings)

[^cite_note-Isaac.Cathedral-335]

&#x20;in the world

[^cite_note-336]

Romania is a [secular state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state) and has no [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion). An overwhelming majority of the population identify themselves as Christians. At the country's 2021 census,[^cite_note-Census2021-Religion-3] 73.60% of respondents identified as [Orthodox Christians](10186.md), with 73.42% belonging to the [Romanian Orthodox Church](384458.md). Other denominations include [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Romania#Protestantism) (6.22%), [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Romania) (3.89%), and [Greek Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Greek_Catholic_Church) (0.61%). From the remaining population 128,291 people belong to other [Christian denominations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_denomination) or have another religion, which includes 58,347 [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Romania) (mostly of [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_language) and [Tatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars) ethnicity) and 2,708 [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Romania) (Jews once constituted 4% of the Romanian population—728,115 persons in the 1930 census). Additionally, 71,430 people are [irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious), 57,229 are [atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist), 25,485 are [agnostic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism), and 2,658,165 people chose to not declare their religion.[^cite_note-Census2021-Religion-3]

The Romanian Orthodox Church is an [autocephalous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autocephalous) [Eastern Orthodox Church](10186.md) in [full communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_communion) with other Orthodox churches, with a [Patriarch](1795665.md) as its leader. It is the third-largest Eastern Orthodox Church in the world,[^cite_note-337] and unlike other Orthodox churches, it functions within a [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) culture and uses a [Romance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_languages) liturgical language.[^cite_note-338] Its canonical jurisdiction covers the territories of Romania and [Moldova](19260.md).[^cite_note-339] Romania has the [world's third-largest Eastern Orthodox population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy_by_country).[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Romania)



The [University of Bucharest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bucharest) was opened in 1864.

Since the [Romanian revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_revolution) of 1989, the [Romanian educational system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Romania) has been in a continuous process of reform that has received mixed criticism.[^cite_note-342] In 2004, some 4.4 million individuals were enrolled in [school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School). Of these, 650,000 were in [kindergarten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kindergarten) (three-six years), 3.11 million in [primary](25058.md) and [secondary](554992.md) level, and 650,000 in tertiary level ([universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University)).[^cite_note-343] In 2018, the adult [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy) was 98.8%.[^cite_note-344] Kindergarten is optional between three and five years. Since 2020, [compulsory schooling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education) starts at age 5 with the last year of kindergarten (grupa mare) and is compulsory until twelfth grade.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] Primary and secondary education is divided into 12 or 13 grades. There is also a semi-legal, informal [private tutoring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutoring#Peer_tutoring) system used mostly during secondary school, which prospered during the [Communist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state).[^cite_note-347]

As of 2025, [Babeș-Bolyai University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babe%C8%99-Bolyai_University) of [Cluj-Napoca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluj-Napoca) and the [University of Bucharest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bucharest) are included in the [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings)' top 800.[^cite_note-348]

Romania ranks fifth in the all-time medal count at the [International Mathematical Olympiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Mathematical_Olympiad) with 316 total medals, dating back to 1959. [Ciprian Manolescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciprian_Manolescu) managed to write a perfect paper (42 points) for a [gold medal](526488.md) more times than anybody else in the history of the competition, in 1995, 1996 and 1997.[^cite_note-349] Romania has achieved the highest team score in the competition, after [China](5405.md), [Russia](25391.md), the [United States](3434750.md) and [Hungary](13275.md). Romania also ranks sixth in the all-time medal count at the [International Olympiad in Informatics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Olympiad_in_Informatics) with 107 total medals, dating back to 1989.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

### Health

Main article: [Health in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Romania)

See also: [Healthcare in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Romania)



The Colțea Hospital in Bucharest completed a \$90 million renovation in 2011

[^cite_note-353]

Healthcare in Romania is mainly provided by the public sector, which runs most hospitals and offers national health insurance to nearly all citizens. In 2021, healthcare costs were US\$16. 7 billion, or US\$2,385 per person, making up €5.69 of GDP. Government spending is higher than in markets like [Bulgaria](3415.md) but lower than [Hungary](13275.md). Spending is expected to rise by 7. US\$5 billion (+37. 68%) from 2024 to 2028, reaching 27. US\$3 billion by 2028.[^cite_note-w828-354]

The [Romanian National Institute of Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_\(Romania\)) reports over 65,000 health units in Romania, with 53,000 in [urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_area) and 12,000 in [rural areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_area). There are 543 [hospitals](18994221.md), including 488 in urban and 55 in rural areas, along with 160 other hospital-like establishments. Nearly 50% of these are large facilities with over 100 beds, while 39% are small with fewer than 50 beds. The total number of inpatient beds is 135,085, allocated mainly to [psychiatry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychiatry), [surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surgery), and [internal medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_medicine) among other specialties.[^cite_note-i223-355]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Romania)

See also: [National symbols of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Romania)

### Holidays and traditions

See also: [Romanian traditional clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_traditional_clothing) and [Folklore of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_of_Romania)



The Christmas market in [Sibiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sibiu) is one of the most famous in Europe.

There are 12 non-working public holidays, including the [Great Union Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Union_Day), celebrated on 1 December in commemoration of the 1918 union of [Transylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transylvania) with Romania.[^cite_note-356] [Winter holidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_and_holiday_season) include the [Christmas](6237.md) and [New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Year) festivities during which various unique [folklore dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore) and [games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game) are common: *[plugușorul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plugu%C8%99orul)*, *[sorcova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorcova)*, *ursul*, and *capra*.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] The traditional [Romanian traditional clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_traditional_clothing) that otherwise has largely fallen out of use during the 20th century, is a popular ceremonial vestment worn on these [festivities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festival), especially in [rural areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_area).[^cite_note-359] There are sacrifices of live [pigs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pig) during Christmas and lambs during [Easter](9325.md) that has required a special exemption from [EU](9317.md) law after 2007.[^cite_note-360] In the Easter, traditions such as [painting the eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_egg) are very common. On 1 March *_[mărțișor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%83r%C8%9Bi%C8%99or)_* gifting is featured, which is a [tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tradition) whereby females are gifted with a type of [talisman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talisman) that is given for [good luck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luck).[^cite_note-361]

### Arts and monuments

Main articles: [Romanian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_literature), [Cinema of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Romania), [Music of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Romania), and [List of World Heritage Sites in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Romania)

See also: [List of films shot in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_shot_in_Romania)

**Architecture**



[Sibiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sibiu) was the 2007 [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture) and the 2019 [European Region of Gastronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Region_of_Gastronomy).

The topic of the origin of [Romanian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Romania) began to be discussed by the end of the 18th century among the [Transylvanian School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transylvanian_School) scholars.[^cite_note-iciculture-362] Several writers rose to prominence in the 19th century, including: [George Coșbuc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Co%C8%99buc), [Ioan Slavici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ioan_Slavici), [Mihail Kogălniceanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihail_Kog%C4%83lniceanu), [Vasile Alecsandri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasile_Alecsandri), [Nicolae Bălcescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_B%C4%83lcescu), [Ion Luca Caragiale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Luca_Caragiale), [Ion Creangă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Creang%C4%83), and [Mihai Eminescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihai_Eminescu), the later being considered the greatest and most influential Romanian poet, particularly for the poem *[Luceafărul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luceaf%C4%83rul_\(poem\))*.[^cite_note-363]

In the 20th century, a number of Romanian artists and writers achieved international acclaim, including: [Tristan Tzara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tristan_Tzara), [Marcel Janco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcel_Janco),[^cite_note-364] [Mircea Eliade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mircea_Eliade), [Nicolae Grigorescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Grigorescu), [Marin Preda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_Preda), [Liviu Rebreanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liviu_Rebreanu),[^cite_note-365] [Eugène Ionesco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne_Ionesco), [Emil Cioran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emil_Cioran), and [Constantin Brâncuși](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantin_Br%C3%A2ncu%C8%99i). Brâncuși has a sculptural ensemble in [Târgu Jiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A2rgu_Jiu), while his sculpture *[Bird in Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_in_Space)*, was auctioned in 2005 for \$27.5 million.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] Romanian-born [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust) survivor [Elie Wiesel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elie_Wiesel) received the [Nobel Peace Prize](26230922.md) in 1986, while [Banat Swabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banat_Swabian) writer [Herta Müller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herta_M%C3%BCller) received the 2009 [Nobel Prize in Literature](23385442.md).[^cite_note-368]



[Timișoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timi%C8%99oara) was designated the [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture) in 2021 and held this title in 2023 due to [COVID-19](62750956.md) postponement.

Prominent Romanian painters include: [Nicolae Grigorescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Grigorescu), [Ștefan Luchian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C8%98tefan_Luchian), [Ion Andreescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Andreescu) [Nicolae Tonitza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Tonitza), and [Theodor Aman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_Aman). Notable Romanian classical composers of the 19th and 20th centuries include: [Ciprian Porumbescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciprian_Porumbescu), [Anton Pann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Pann), [Eduard Caudella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Caudella), [Mihail Jora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihail_Jora), [Dinu Lipatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinu_Lipatti), and especially [George Enescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Enescu). The annual [George Enescu Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Enescu_Festival) is held in Bucharest in honour of the 20th-century composer.[^cite_note-369]

Contemporary musicians like [Angela Gheorghiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Gheorghiu), [Gheorghe Zamfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gheorghe_Zamfir),[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] [Inna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inna),[^cite_note-372] [Alexandra Stan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandra_Stan),[^cite_note-373] and many others have achieved various levels of international acclaim. From the late 2000s through the early 2010s, the Romanian [popcorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popcorn_\(Romanian_music_style\)) music style had established itself in the [international mainstream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainstream_media).[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] At the [Eurovision Song Contest](9954.md) Romanian singers achieved third place in 2005 and 2010.[^cite_note-376]

In cinema, several movies of the [Romanian New Wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_New_Wave) have achieved international acclaim. At the [Cannes Film Festival](101460.md), *[The Death of Mr. Lazarescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Death_of_Mr._Lazarescu)* by [Cristi Puiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristi_Puiu) won the *[Prix Un Certain Regard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Un_Certain_Regard#Prix_Un_Certain_Regard_winners)* in 2005,[^cite_note-377] while *[4 Months, 3 Weeks and 2 Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_Months,_3_Weeks_and_2_Days)* by [Cristian Mungiu](11409466.md) won the festival's top prize, the *[Palme d'Or](612946.md)*, in 2007.[^cite_note-378] At the [Berlin International Film Festival](18948039.md), *[Child's Pose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child's_Pose_\(film\))* by [Călin Peter Netzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83lin_Peter_Netzer) won the [Golden Bear](21547166.md) in 2013.[^cite_note-379]

The list of [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) includes [six cultural sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Romania) located within Romania, including eight [painted churches of northern Moldavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_churches_of_northern_Moldavia), eight wooden churches of [Maramureș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maramure%C8%99), seven villages with fortified churches in [Transylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transylvania), the [Horezu Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horezu_Monastery), and the [Historic Centre of Sighișoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Centre_of_Sighi%C8%99oara).[^cite_note-380] The city of Sibiu, with its [Brukenthal National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brukenthal_National_Museum), was selected as the 2007 [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture) and the 2019 [European Region of Gastronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Region_of_Gastronomy).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] Multiple castles exist in Romania, including the popular tourist attractions of [Peleș Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pele%C8%99_Castle),[^cite_note-383] [Corvin Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corvin_Castle), and [Bran Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bran_Castle) or "Dracula's Castle".[^cite_note-384]

### Media

Main article: [Media of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Romania)

### Cuisine

Main article: [Romanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_cuisine)

[Romanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_cuisine) has been influenced by [Austrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_cuisine) and [German cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_cuisine) (especially in the historical regions that had been formerly administered by the [Habsburg monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy)), but also shares some similarities with other cuisines in the [Balkan region](4829.md) such as [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_cuisine), [Bulgarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarian_cuisine), and [Serbian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_cuisine).[^cite_note-385] *[Ciorbă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciorb%C4%83)* includes a wide range of [sour soups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sour_soup), while *_[mititei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mititei)_*, *_[mămăligă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%83)_* (similar to [polenta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polenta)), and *[sarmale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarma_\(food\))* are featured commonly in [main courses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_course).[^cite_note-386]

[Pork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pork), [chicken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicken), and [beef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef) are the preferred types of [meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meat), but [lamb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_and_mutton) and [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish) are also quite popular.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] Certain traditional recipes are made in direct connection with the [holidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiday): *_[chiftele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kofta)_*, *_[tobă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tob%C4%83)_* and *_[tochitură](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tochitur%C4%83)_* at Christmas; *_[drob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drob)_*, *_[pască](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paska_\(bread\))_* and *_[cozonac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cozonac)_* at Easter and other Romanian holidays.[^cite_note-389] *[Țuică](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C8%9Auic%C4%83)* is a strong plum [brandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandy) reaching a 70% alcohol content which is the country's traditional [alcoholic beverage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcoholic_beverage), taking as much as 75% of the national crop (Romania is one of the [largest plum producers in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_plum_production)).[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-educations-391] Traditional alcoholic beverages also include [wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_wine), *[rachiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachiu)*, *[palincă](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palinc%C4%83)* and *[vișinată](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vi%C8%99inat%C4%83)*, but [beer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_in_Romania) consumption has increased dramatically over recent years.[^cite_note-392] In 2019, Romania became the country with the [highest alcohol consumption per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_alcohol_consumption_per_capita).

### Sports

Main article: [Sport in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Romania)



[Arena Națională](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arena_Na%C8%9Bional%C4%83), opened in 2011, the national stadium of Romania, as seen on a Romanian stamp (2011)

[Football](10568.md) is the most popular sport in Romania with over 219,000 registered players as of 2018. The market for professional football in Romania was roughly €740 million in 2018 according to [UEFA](32332.md).[^cite_note-393]

The governing body is the [Romanian Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Football_Federation), which belongs to UEFA. The [Romania national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania_national_football_team) played its first match in 1922 and is one of only four national teams to have taken part in the first three [FIFA World Cups](11370.md), the other three being [Brazil](3383.md), [France](5843419.md), and [Belgium](3343.md). Overall, it has played in seven World Cups and had its most successful period during the 1990s, when it finished 6th at the [1994 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup), eventually being ranked 3rd by [FIFA](11049.md) in 1997.[^cite_note-394]

The most successful club is [Steaua București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSA_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti_\(football\)), who were the first Eastern European team to win the [UEFA Champions League](44220.md) in 1986, and were runners-up in 1989.[^cite_note-395] [Dinamo București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti) reached the UEFA Champions League semi-final in 1984 and the [UEFA Cup Winners' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners'_Cup) semi-final in [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989%E2%80%9390_European_Cup_Winners'_Cup).[^cite_note-396] Other important Romanian football clubs are [Rapid București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Rapid_Bucure%C8%99ti),[^cite_note-397] [UTA Arad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTA_Arad),[^cite_note-398] [Universitatea Craiova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Universitatea_Craiova),[^cite_note-399] [Petrolul Ploiești](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrolul_Ploie%C8%99ti),[^cite_note-400] [CFR Cluj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CFR_Cluj),[^cite_note-401] [Astra Giurgiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_Giurgiu),[^cite_note-402] and [Viitorul Constanța](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viitorul_Constan%C8%9Ba)[^cite_note-403] (the latter having recently merged with [FCV Farul Constanța](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FCV_Farul_Constan%C8%9Ba)).[^cite_note-404]

Tennis is the second most popular sport.[^cite_note-Hotnews-405] Romania reached the [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup) finals three times in 1969, 1971 and 1972.[^cite_note-406] The second most popular [team sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_sport) is [handball](13730.md).[^cite_note-Hotnews-405] The [men's team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania_men's_national_handball_team) won the [handball world championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Men's_Handball_Championship) in [1961](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1961_World_Men's_Handball_Championship), [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_World_Men's_Handball_Championship), [1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_World_Men's_Handball_Championship), [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_World_Men's_Handball_Championship) making them the third most successful nation ever in the tournament. The [women's team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania_women's_national_handball_team) won the [world championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IHF_World_Women's_Handball_Championship) in [1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_World_Women's_Handball_Championship) and have enjoyed more success than their male counterparts in recent years. In the club competition Romanian teams have won the [EHF Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EHF_Champions_League) a total of three times, [Steaua București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSA_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti_\(handball\)) won in [1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967%E2%80%9368_European_Cup_\(handball\)) as well as [1977](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976%E2%80%9377_European_Cup_\(handball\)) and [Dinamo București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_\(men's_handball\)) won in [1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964%E2%80%9365_European_Cup_\(handball\)).[^cite_note-407] In women's handball, powerhouse [CSM București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSM_Bucure%C8%99ti_\(women's_handball\)) lifted the [EHF Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_EHF_Champions_League) trophy in 2016.[^cite_note-408]

Popular [individual sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_sport) include [combat sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_sport),[^cite_note-Hotnews-405] martial arts,[^cite_note-Hotnews-405] and swimming.[^cite_note-Hotnews-405] In [professional boxing](4283645.md), Romania has produced many world champions across the weight divisions internationally recognised by governing bodies.[^cite_note-409] Another popular combat sport is [professional kickboxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_kickboxing), which has produced prominent practitioners including.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411]

Romania's 306 [all-time Summer Olympics medals](3729318.md) would rank 12th most among all countries, while its 89 [gold medals](526488.md) would be 14th most. The [1984 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics) was their most successful run, where they won 53 medals in total, 20 of them gold, ultimately placing 2nd to the hosts [United States](3434750.md) in the [medal rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics_medal_table). Amongst countries who have never hosted the event themselves, they are second in the total number of medals earned.[^cite_note-412] [Gymnastics](231482.md) is the country's major medal-producing sport.[^cite_note-413]
